## Meryth Heartwood

Meryth Heartwood was a spellscarred resident of Helm's Hold and a member of the Heirs of Azure political movement that arose in the city during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Unlike the more extreme followers of the movement, Meryth advocated for peaceful resistance against the anti-spellscared sentiments that were so strong among her fellow citizens.

Personality
Meryth held the culture of Illefarn with great reverence, particularly the form of artistry known as song paths, portal networks associated with performances of hours-long poems.

History
Meryth was a devout believer in the movement led by the half-elf spellscarred Halas, as well as the all-inclusive oration offered by the Prophet Rohini. She was wholly disillusioned when Rohini revealed herself to be the leader of the Ashmadai, and cleansed Halas and many of his followers of their spellscars, in exchange for their devotion to the devil-worshiping Ashmadai.

During the Siege of Helm's Hold, circa the, Meryth joined a resistance group operating at a camp at the edge of the Watcher's Forest. She found comrades among the group, including her fellow elf Dassar Truebow, a hedge wizard named Nottin, and the merchant Kella the Fair-Dealing. She worked with fellow elven adventurers to help recover stolen relics from Illefarn, and prevent the Ashmadai from opening asong paths within the city.

## Merchants' Guild

The Merchants' Guild was a guild of merchants in Cormyr active in the mid–14 century DR.

Organization
Membership was divided up into districts of Cormyr, each with its own representative.

The richest and most influential merchants formed its governing body, the Grand Council. Their titles included Council President and Council Members, while special guests were just that, Council Special Guests.

Activities
Members paid money to the guild, but the benefits were intangible. Hence not all merchants and business-owners in Cormyr chose to join.

The Grand Council held an annual meeting. Typically, they convened in one of Cormyr's major cities, namely Suzail, Marsember, or Arabel, but sometimes they went to a smaller resort town like Gladehap. Members could lobby to host the annual meeting in their own towns, provided they could arrange facilities, accommodation, and quality food and drink. It was a costly enterprise, but also a boost to local economy. On arrival, councilors were gifted a ceremonial food and drink of welcome, such as a piece of elven bread from Evermeet and fresh water in a silver goblet, and a sampling of local produce and crafts in sack woven from silvery mesh. Guests were also granted silver medallions (about 5 sp for the metal alone) from which hung parchments bearing their name, origin, and position in the guild.

History
For the 1367 DR meeting of the Grand Council, Barthelm Meadowbrock arranged to host it in his own town of Ghars, and this was quite a coup for him. He booked the Sheaf of Wheat and Silver Scythe inns to lodge guild members and their retinues (there were too many for just one inn) and even paid for a meeting room to be added to the Silver Scythe. Moreover, he managed provisions, arranged for drinks through the Bold Bard tavern, and brought chefs from Suzail. It was the most exciting thing to happen to Ghars; it puffed up local merchants and drew agents of the Iron Throne and Zhentarim; the local Purple Dragons arrested one of the former and two of the latter in the two months preceding. Captain Flim of the Purple Dragons oversaw security in Ghars and Mayor Tobald the town's welcome.

In the lead-up, townsfolk tidied the streets, washed shop windows, put garlands and wreaths on lampposts, and hung welcoming banners. Council members and their retinues arrived mid-afternoon of Eleint 19. Mayella Meadowbrock, Barthelm's daughter, presented each with their gifts and Barthelm gave a welcome speech. The Sheaf of Wheat hosted a grand reception that night. This was buffet dining, with stag sausage, lamb stew, an oyster and wild rice dish, drinks of Suzale and Elminster's Choice, with the main course being Beef and Oysters Barnabas with Westgate Ruby. The main meeting was to be hosted in the Silver Scythe on Eleint 20. Barthelm likely left the Grand Council members blissfully unaware of a foiled plot by the Iron Throne to assassinate them by poisoning the town water supply with blackweed.

Known Members
 Barthelm Meadowbrock, Grand Council member and district representative for the area of Ghars.

## Menhir

Menhirs were a type of stone formation typically used for portals or outdoor religious shrines.

History
In ancient times, the Yuir folk of Aglarond used menhir stones that were infused with crystals to create monumental circles that they performed mystical ceremonies in and used as portals. These menhirs all bore inscriptions that were carved in their ancient Elven dialect. Over time the power of these menhirs faded and they became buried within the undergrowth of Yuirwood, overgrown with grass or moss. Those that retained their old magic glowed unearthly colors under the light of the full moon. Some became used as meeting places by fey creatures, such as pixies and sprites.

The menhir circles that remained known of within the Yuirwood formed a portal network that the Masters of the Yuirwood, an elite group of half-elven druids and rangers, used to rapidly move across the woods to respond to any growing threats. Beyond the forest, they could even travel to the realm of Sildëyuir.

In 1359 DR, Zembrath Klun discovered a menhir on Mezeketh Isle that was inscribed in an ancient Jhaamdathan script.

During the Spellplague, many of the menhir circles of Yuirwood seemed to attract active pockets of the eponymous plague.

In 1479 DR,The date for all Year 1 RPGA sanctioned adventures is considered to be 1479 DR for the purposes of this wiki. the Festering Wound tribe conducted a mining operation within the Tannath Mountains on behalf of a mage known as the Saffron Devil, harvesting within its depths the same menhir stones that were once used by the Yuir folk. They attached the mined stones to sledges, which they then had pulled by giant ants.

Notable Menhirs

 A menhir stood just outside the city of Relkath's Foot.
 Temples of Callarduran Smoothhands customarily had a solitary menhir as their central altar. These were fashioned from stalagmites and inlaid with hundreds of tiny rubies.
 The nation of Amn was host to a great number of standing stone formations, including the Five Silvers, the Giants' Dance, the Prayer Stone, the Seventeen Sentries, and Waukeen's Circle.
 The Black obelisks, a series of magical obelisks originating from ancient Netheril that possessed various forms of incredible power.
 Calimban Knoll was a circle in the Calim Desert consisting of twenty-one menhirs.
 Circle of Thunder, a circular arrangement of menhirs atop a hill in Neverwinter Wood.
 Fang of Skulls
 Flint Rock had four menhirs encircling its altar, each of which pointed in one of the cardinal directions.
 The Phylund Hunting Lodge's northern section had a large menhir, haphazardly carved with symbols and oaths to Tempus and Malar, forming much of its southwest corner.
 In the forest near the Coast Way crossing stood six ancient menhirs that posed riddles to passersby.
 In the Ice Spires, the kingdom of Hartsvale had the boundaries of it and the neighboring giant lands marked by titanic menhirs, which some speculated were placed there by stone giants.
 Illith'arear, an ancient druidic circle of redwood trees with rune-engraved menhirs embedded within them.
 Ironmaster Vale's borders were marked by menhirs, each inscribed with the coat-of-arms of Ironmaster.
 The plains of Jotunheim were dotted with many ancient menhirs.
 The plateaus of Raven Rock were dotted with a number of menhirs.
 The Speaking Stones was a menhir circle in Blingdenstone, around which the original city was built.
 Stone Stand's two cairn rings were each surmounted by a ring of menhirs.
 The Twenty Stones of Thruun comprised a score of vertical granite menhirs.
 Within Undermountain, the Caverns of Ooze had a pair of menhirs that acted as a portal to the Terminus Level.
 The Wyvernstones of Hullack, a group of menhirs at a site sacred to Eldath in the Hullack Forest.
 Spear Rock, a shrine to Tempus in the western foothills of the Storm Horns.

Rumor & Legends
Some claimed that the menhir circles of Yuirwood could transport one to Evermeet.

## Medechai

Medechai was a medusa raised by the Baron of Blood, Artor Morlin, who then turned her into his chosen vampire spawn when she reached adulthood. She lived in the Fireplace Level beneath the North Ward of Waterdeep circa the .

Description
Her skin was covered in scales similar to a yuan-ti and was the color of soil. Her face was hideous, and vampirism made her red eyes glow an even deeper shade of red. Her mouth did not exhibit fangs, but each of the lethargic snakes on her head had a pair that could deliver a vampire bite. She wore a tight-fitting emerald green chain shirt.

Personality
Artor trained her to be an icy-blooded killer that went for the quick victory rather than toy with her prey. The only time she displayed emotion was when drinking blood—her snakes hissed and writhed in sinuous ecstasy. She had very little knowledge of the world outside of the Fireplace Level that was her home.

Abilities
Medechai had all the strengths and weaknesses of a vampire, but as Artor Morlin's chosen spawn, she was exceptionally strong and dexterous as long as he was not destroyed. In addition, her assassin training allowed her to cast spells such as invisibility, cat's grace, and true strike. She could still user her petrifying gaze, if necessary,  and could fire her bow at point-blank range.

Possessions
Her armor was a +3 mithral chain shirt and she had a +2 buckler. Her melee weapon was known as Gravebite, a +1 ghost touch keen dagger, and her bow was called Thunderbolt, a +1 thundering composite shortbow.

Her coffin was actually a large block of greenish-brown stone that was carved in the shape of a helmet, about  in diameter. She had her fellow spawn, Arrick Kaarvol, hollow out the area behind the eyes (using stone shape) and coat them in shiny mithral. The entrance to this hidden chamber were two small holes, one in each eyelid, that she could access while in gaseous form. The gigantic helm floated off the floor in a corner of Unger Farshal's storeroom.

Activities
Her primary directive from Artor Morlin was to stay within the Ghost Cage that surrounded the Fireplace Level and guard against intruders on both the Prime Material plane and the Ethereal plane. She tended to lurk while interlopers searched her lair and then attack when they were less vigilant or weakened.

Relationships
Medechai hated Artor Morlin, but was bound to obey him until one of them was destroyed.

History
Morlin found Medechai in the slave market of Skullport sometime around 1342 DR. He raised her from childhood, keeping her under his domination until she reached adulthood, then turned her into a vampire. She was kept mostly isolated from the outside world, but found the giant helmed head while wandering outside the Ghost Cage and brought it home to become her coffin.The source describes this as the head of a fallen Walking Statue of Waterdeep, but as of 1372 DR no known statue had fallen and the scale of the artifact is too small, considering that the entire shop and residence of Thort's Findings fit inside the head of The Swordmaiden (see Waterdeep: Dragon Heist, p. 184). However, City of Splendors: Waterdeep describes the walking statues as "almost forty feet tall" (see p. 140) which is more reasonable to have a head and helmet  in diameter.

## Metos

Metos was a fortress in the Methwood built by the Imaskari in . It was made famous by Hilather and Madryoch, who had their showdown in it.

History
Metos had, when created, sufficient extra-dimensional space to hold five-thousand soldiers and still be self-sufficient. It was very well protected against infiltration, as it required a portal key to access. The fortress was where Madryoch was confronted by a young Hilather, before being banished to the Demiplane of Imprisonment.

However, the strength of the complex was never tested. Lord Yuvaraj recalled the forces from the complex to quash the rebellion of the Mulan in −2489 to −2488 DR, leaving the fortress virtually unmanned, confident no one would know how to access its portals.

Legends
It was said that Turami refugees displaced by the Mulan slaves dwelled in Metos for centuries before migrating westwards to Chessenta, Unther, and Turmish.

## Mercenary Army of Krak al-Niraan

The Mercenary Army of Krak al-Niraan was the strong arm of the Brotherhood of the True Flame in Zakhara in 1367 DR.

Organization
Funded by Jamal al-Yindannim's impressive coffers, the Mercenary Army did not have trouble attracting new recruits. All new recruits needed to first pass a security check conducted by Ali al-Lazan–Jamal's Chief Inquisitor. Soldiers who passed the inspection had a geas placed upon them to ensure their complete loyalty.

The strength of the Army's forces as of 1367 DR is listed below:
 10 Captains
 20 Lieutenants
 40 Sergeants
 50 Heavy infantry
 70 Medium infantry
 350 Light infantry
 150 Crossbowmen
 100 Archers
 200 Light cavalry

The heavy infantry of the Army was composed completely of skilled hill giants and the medium infantry of ogres.

Activities
The Army was maintained in battle-ready status by executing continuous raids on the al-Badia of the Haunted Lands and Furrowed Mountains.

Base of Operations
The Army was based in the fortress of Krak al-Niraan within the Weeping Desert of the Haunted Lands, though only a quarter of the forces were present within the fortress at any given time since most of the soldiers were out raiding.

Members
Barzan al-Hiyali was the captain and commander of the Army. A brilliant warmonger genie named Khaizuran devised all of the Army's strategy.

## Merchant Guild Hall

The Merchant Guild Hall in the River District of Neverwinter was the guildhall of a merchant's guild that operated in the city in the early-to-mid 15 century DR. It was a fort of the Many-Arrows orcs under Vansi Bloodscar in the mid-to-late 15 century DR.

Location
The guildhall was located in Neverwinter's old Merchant Square.

Structure
The Many-Arrows orcs made rough-and-ready repairs to the ruined guildhall, propping up the collapsing ceilings with wooden beams and patching the holes in the roof with planks and hides. Fortifying it, they girded it with ramshackle wooden towers and stockades, all of wood and hide.

Interior

Within, the orcs had contemptuously burned, smashed, or defiled all the existing artworks within and hung their banners and hides, horns and skulls in their place. They repurposed the old meeting halls, dining rooms, and storerooms for their own purposes. It was filled with debris, supplies, and weaponry. Lighting was provided by torches where sunlight didn't shine through holes in the roof.

History
Originally, the guildhall was an opulent place, adorned with high-quality tapestries and paintings such as "The Prophet". Leaders of the merchant guild came here to negotiate trade and Neverwinter's prosperity, as well as to live in luxury.

However, following the Mount Hotenow eruption in 1451 DR, the majority of surviving merchants fled the ruined city and resettled in Waterdeep. Others relocated to the Neverwinter's Protector's Enclave after the trade market was re-established there by Lord Dagult Neverember. Their former guildhall remained abandoned and fell into ruin.

Then, when a tribe of orcs from Many-Arrows invaded the River District in the late 1470s DR, they seized the Merchant Guild Hall and made it their fortress.

Notable Inhabitants
 Kartok Paingiver, an orc warband leader

## Men of the Red Kestrel

The Men of the Red Kestrel were a group of stalwart adventurers, active circa 1358 DR.

History
In 1358 DR, the Men of the Red Kestrel sought a way through the Great Glacier to the east from the Bloodstone Lands. When they arrived at the village of Sundice, they shared stories of encountering dwarves, frozen within the Great Glacier. These mysterious dwarves wore exquisitely crafted sets of armor and weapons, unseen within the Realms of the 14 century DR. The adventurers brought two of these pieces of rare weaponry along from the trip across the Glacier, a long sword and a warhammer of great quality.

Both items were bought by Eldaerim of Sossal, who decided to become a sponsor for the Men of the Red Kestrel's next foray into the Great Glacier.

## Medinat Muskawoon

Medinat Muskatwoon was a small city in the kingdom of Bakar, around the lake Me'at Halwa. It became a ghost city not long after the river dried; the wizard Martek used its ruins to hide the Star of Melos-Pelar.

Description
The limestone causeway narrowed closer to the city, but never became narrower than . It led into the northern city square: there stood an obelisk,  tall and  at its base, with several inscriptions by Martek cryptically stating the way to recover the Star of Melos-Pelar. The causeway ran between the ruins of the Medinat Muskatwoon government building, and the Pasha's palace. East of the causeway, in an otherwise clear area, only two buildings remained recognizable in any way: the Temple of Horus and the Temple of Prometheus.

West from the causeway, counter-clockwise from the center, lay a ruined egyptian-style Temple of Osiris, a ruined Finnish temple of Untamo, the Baths of Drusus, and a ruined celtic temple of Dunatis. The Temple of Balder in the city still stood. Continuing counter-clockwise one could find a Roman-style sandstone hippodrome, and a greek-style black marble Collegium; both were thoroughly ruined, but still stood somewhat recognizable. The Temple of Ra was next, followed by ruined baths; almost due south from the center of the lake was another building full of baths, known as the Baths of Persepolis.

On the south-eastern quadrant were a Palaestra, three arabic brick caravanserai, another bath known as the Baths of Kordek, and finally a ruined greek temple of Tyche.

Location
The City of Medinat Muskatwoon lay on the banks of the lake Me'at Halwa, in the middle of five hills in the Raurin.

History
The natural limestone roadbed on the northwest of Medinat Muskatwoon's hills made it a natural location for a city. Travelers from all over the world at the time brought their wares to Medinatin before carrying them through the rest of Bakar. Though it never grew past a square mile, Medinat Muskatwoon grew into a thriving cultural hub, becoming a metropolis on its own right. After the death of Amun-Re around the year  the river Athis stopped flowing and the Me'at soon dried as well, the small streams that fed it becoming insufficient without the underground aquifers that the Athis fed. Without the river, trade across the desert also dried up, and thus travel became ever more expensive. Gradually this made it more and more difficult to relocate from the city, encouraging the gouging of the inhabitants, leaving at every turn only the poorest and most desperate.

The last caravan to arrive became overwhelmed by a violent mob of frenzied, desperate citizens, craving water so badly as to drink the blood of the camels. The remaining population died not long after. Medinat Muskatwoon became a ghost town, visited by adventurers looking for treasure and magical items; they returned telling stories of ghosts that craved moisture, and other kinds of undead. This is how Medinat Muskatwoon came to the name of "Cursed City of Stone".

50 years after the last inhabitants walked the city, the efreet Pasha Khalitharius took up residence there, organizing the undead of the city into his army, the Malatath. At some point after that, at least 1000 years before  (approximately the year ) the wizard Martek came to the city together with a small caravan to challenge and stop the efreet. The efreet's army waged a slow, torturous war of attrition on the wizard's caravan, slaying one of their guards every night, then their camels, then draining their water, until Martek finally confronted the efreet in single combat. He emerged victorious, but the flames burned the city, and in fact, the surrounding area, for 20 years after their fight.

Martek decided to turn the ruins of the city into the hiding place for the Star of Melos-Pelar, creating with his magic a number of puzzles based on the temples of the gods in the city.

Notable Buildings
Temple of Ra
Temple of Balder
Temple of Horus
Temple of Prometheus
Baths of Drusus
Baths of Persepolis
Baths of Kordek

Ruined Baths
Only one of five vaulted roofs held aloft by simple columns remained now, covering a large circular pool. As of the time of Khalitarius' release, the body of a dead adventurer lay close to the pool, holding a golden object in their hand. Three wraiths dwelt there.

Collegium
A collegium's facade still endured; it was made of black marble. Only part of the back half of the building endured still, most of its domes gone. Near the base of an outside wall, some slate had been attached; a unique spectre lectured there on the subject of the meter of poetry in an ancient language. Though dull, it could answer questions about the city, though it wouldn't answer more than one question per person, instead hurling invective at anyone who interrupted its lecture twice.

Palaestra
The palaestra was a square building  on a side made of grey-pink stone, covered in attractive frescoes of young men in various games and gymnastic activities. It had arched entrances on the north and south walls, and a peaked clay-tile roof, longer on the south end than on the north. Inside, it had a peristyle courtyard and a pool, now dry, which was inhabited by centipedes as of the mid-14 century DR. In its time it provided a colonnaded area for lounging, reading, and athletics. Three golden links were hidden there.

Hazards
Apparitions of the city in its time and undead were almost the only creatures populating the zone, save for a smattering of centipedes in the Palaestra. The undead included ghosts of a mob, thieves, or fighters, wights, and wraiths.

## Merrygold Brightshine

Merrygold Brightshine was a halfling priest of Lathander and the caretaker of the Shrine of Lathander in Leilon in the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Merrygold was outgoing, amiable, and quick to take care of those in need. He could be a bit reckless when he thought that there was good to be done.

Activities
As the caretaker of Leilon's Shrine of Lathander, Merrygold led religious services each dawn and presided over a small number of fellow faithful. Under his stewardship, the Shrine took on the role of caring for the laborers and soldiers from Neverwinter who were participating in the reconstruction of the town. He personally provided healing and restoration services in exchange for donations to Lathander.

Relationships
Merrygold was much beloved by the people of Leilon.

History
When efforts to rebuild Leilon began, the Church of Lathander eagerly donated to the project in order to open the first temple in the reclaimed town. Merrygold thus had the honor of overseeing the rebuilding and restoration of Leilon's original Shrine of Lathander, which made him one of the original founders of the new town at Leilon. With the adoration of the people cementing his importance in the community, he soon became a member of the town council.

When Leilon came under attack from the Cult of Talos and the townsfolk and laborers were driven out, Merrygold called for his fellow councilors to act fast to find a pair of missing children without regard for the dangers, putting him at odds with the head of the council, Grizzelda Copperwraught.

## Me'at Halwa

The Me'at Halwa was a lake in Raurin. After the pharaoh Amun-Re died, his curse dried it out; the fight between Martek and Khalitharius filled its dry banks with molten glass, rendering it part of the Skysea.

Overview
The Me'at Halwa was formed by the waters from five surrounding small hills. Much of the city of Medinat Muskawoon was built on them, and around the lake. The lake emptied into the river Athis, to flow northwestward and eventually reach the sea.

History
The city of Medinat Muskawoon was built around the Me'at Halwa. After the Curse of Amun-Re took effect, the level of the underground waters, themselves fed by the River Athis, fell enough that the outflow from the surrounding hills was not enough to keep the water in place. Thus, the lake dried, even though it was not fed by the river Athis.

As of, the Me'at Halwa was a part of the Skysea, created after the battle between Martek and Khalitharius. The lake's dry bed, and part of its banks leading towards the Athis, were covered in a layer of glass with an average thickness of .

## Merge with nature

Merge with nature was a powerful divine alteration and necromancy spell used by priestesses devoted to Sune, enabling them to avoid old age by becoming a dryad.

Effects
Priestesses of Sune used this spell to avoid the effects of old age and even death by permanently transforming into a dryad. Only higher-ranking and particularly desperate priestesses used this spell. This spell fell out of favor with the Church of Sune but was retained by the Sunite liturgy.

The caster united herself with a subject tree, allowing her to transform into a dryad. The priestess lost all of her divine abilities in exchange for the powers and skills of a true dryad. The transformation was quite stressful on the priestesses' body, sometimes resulting in their death.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component a tree willing to accept the dryad to cast.

## Merrymar's Merchandise

Merrymar's Merchandise was the only general store in the halfling village of Shining Falls in the Delimbiyr Vale in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. It was owned by the local merchant Blaz Merrymar.

Structure
The building was low and long. It was marked by a placard displaying Blaz's smiling face.

Staff
The store was owned by Blaz Merrymar, but its day-to-day running was handled by his wife Rinyon and their children. Blaz procured goods for the store, at least when he was not engaging in get-rich-quick schemes.

Blaz had exclusive deals with all the crafters in the village so that only he could sell their wares to outsiders. Fortunately, his good judge of character, reasonable commissions from fellow halflings, and fair prices for customers made this monopoly profitable for all.

History
In the summer of, goods meant for Merrymar's Merchandise were raided on the road by bandits, thought to be orcs of the High Forest. Tragically, Blaz and Rinyon lost not only merchandise but also all four of their sons and three nephews, leaving the extended family decimated and the store understaffed.

## Merelith

Lady Merelith was the ruler of Saerloon in Sembia in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
In truth ruling as a figurehead of the city, Merelith answered to a council of wealthy and powerful merchants who really ruled circa . Merelith was also the high captain of the City Guard of Saerloon.

History
During the Twilight War of the, Merelith sided with Overmistress Mirabeta Selkirk and offered her support against the rebels of Selgaunt and Saerb. On Uktar 21, Mirabeta sent the disguised green dragon Vendemniharan to serve under Merelith with orders to lead an army against Selgaunt. On Uktar 29, under Mirabeta orders, Merelith used a teleportation circle to transport her army to just outside Selgaunt in order to attack and take it by surprise.

After the total defeat of her troops during the Battle of Selgaunt and the Shadowstorm consumed Ordulin, Merelith offered her surrender to Thamalon Uskevren II. On the advice of Rivalen Tanthul, he accepted peace with Saerloon but only after Merelith and her court abdicated.

## Merratet

Merratet was one of two divine realms in the Great Wheel cosmology belonging to the Pharaonic and Mulhorandi goddess of cats, Bast.

Cosmography
This divine realm was located within the plane of Ysgard, in the layer of Asgard. It encompassed an earthbeg that was shared with Netaph, the divine realm of Anhur, which was on the far side of the ocean that bordered Vanaheim.
Planar Traits
The most noteworthy peculiarity of this realm was that the dreams of Bast seemed to echo throughout it whenever she slept, dreams of hunting and bloody feasts, and invade the minds of the sleeping. Merratet's local fauna were unphased by these dreams and its petitioners took them rather well, but visitors to the realm found themselves restless and unable to regain their spells, whether they were mages or priests, waking up with the shakes every half hour.

These dreams strangely behaved differently in a city on this plane known as Besiris, involving the dreamer being stalked and killed. Over the course of a few days or weeks, the nightmares would induce a maddening paranoia, causing the dreamer to wall themselves up in their room until they died from lack of either food or water. Those who attempted to help one suffering dream madness were likely to be attacked by them.

Geography
Merratet was largely a wasting desert with a moderate climate, rolling hills, dense cover of thickets, and a dry savannah. There were also watering holds that animals gathered around at, but petitioners were typically too afraid to get near.
Flora & Fauna
The realm was home to a great deal of mighty and sleek felines, such as tigers, who preyed upon plump game animals that roamed it. Though they were also liable to attack a petitioner or other travelers. In the thickets, only the largest of animals were safe from being ambushed.

One of the unique species of feline that inhabited Merratet was a breed of black furred, green eyed housecats called Bast's Children.

Notable Locations
 Bast's home was a large vineyard covered acropolis, located near the edge of Merratet's desert. Bast and the spirits that served her often had festivals, parties, and masquerades going on inside its halls.
 A number of towns existed in this realm. It was rare to find them in the thickets and even rarer to find one in the realm's deserts or dry savannah.
 Eowr, a small village of rather wise and arrogant celestial lammasu, who would tend to anyone that was hurt or sick. The lammasu also frequently spoke of lessons on humility, temperance, and virtue as part of an effort to convert travelers to the cause of Law.
 Rummmm was a town in this realm that gradually developed into a city after its inhabitants discovered that the felines of Merratet would not attack them so long as they wore a mask on the back of their head. They freely sold masks to travelers, often at a price of one gold piece. They were also willing to take in anyone, so long as they could pay their way or provide its inhabitants amusing diversions.
 The most mysterious settlement in Merratet was Bresiris, also known as the town of Dreaming Death.Planes of Chaos alternates between calling Bresiris a city and village. As the description of the location lines up with a city that was briefly detailed, yet unnamed, in On Hallowed Ground that terminology has been given preference. It stood in the center of the realm and was quite ancient in age, its every building sporting cracks or crumbling walls. The city was surrounded in rumor and was wrongfully thought by most Merratians to be haunted. One of the few facts known about Besiris was that any visitors to the city either never left or were driven out with horrible scratches and claw marks. Spoken of in rumors, the city was truly inhabited by werepanthers and ruled by a rakshasa Maharajah along with his sorceress concubine, though rumors only spoke of one or the other. The rulers of Bresiris would either kill or polymorph any dream hunter they found in the city into a pet, slave, or spy that kept track of visitors.

Inhabitants
Bast's realm was inhabited by petitioners and all manner of intelligent felines, such as tabaxi, sphinxes, weretigers, and a few wandering tribes of other varities of feline lycanthrope. The last of whom were known to offer sanctuary to travelers. In addition to those permanent residents, one could ocassionaly find various members of the Norse pantheon hiding in Merratet, such as Bragi and Loki.

The realm was best known for its dream hunters, felines who hunted down the dreams of Bast that plagued people (not the cats) in this realm, ensuring a peaceful sleep. They all possessed a variant form of precognition and spirit sense. Merratians widely acknowledged that the best dream hunters in the realm were typically Bast's Children, sphinxes, and tabaxi. The greatest dream hunters were said to be capable of even killing dreams, fates, prophecies or wyrds.

Dream hunters were common near all of Merratet's entrances and most towns kept one on a payroll to ensure that visitors weren't plagued by Bast's dreams. Some dream hunters hired themselves out to protect groups traveling across the realm, though their rates were steep, mosting charging a fee of twenty gold pieces or more per day. In Breseris they typically struggled against the onslaught of dreams and made only half-hearted attempts to protect dreamers from their madness.

Notable Inhabitants
 Meuronna, a tall and lithe female tabaxi who was one of the dream hunters of this realm.
 Mragatep, an androsphinx that was considered to be one of the greatest dream hunters in Merratet.
 Shinora of the White Mane, the oldest and kindest of the lammasu that inhabited Merratet. She was quite talkative and had a fondness for cloves and sweet wine.
 Skullbury, an enormous and intelligent male panther that could communicate with other creatures. Bast left control of Merratet in his hands whenever she left. He was perhaps best known for his habit of stashing kills within the closets, gardens, and wardrobes of Bast's palace.
 White Shadow, a female weretiger and the leader of a pride of infamous weretiger bandits that operated on the outskirts of Rumm, known as the Half Moon Tigers.

Rumors & Legends
 Some claimed that those restless from the dreams of Bast were more likely to be attacked by the plane's felines.
 There were a number of rumors that circulated about the weretiger known as White Shadow. Some claimed that she had been gradually spending more time in the plane Gates of the Moon, free of her bloodlust. Those in Bast's palace spoke of her having a lover in that realm, having accidentally devoured at least two previous lovers during intercourse in Merratet after transforming into her bestial form.
 It was believed that there existed several gates in Merratet that connected the realm to the Gilded Hall of the Society of Sensation.

Trivia
A book known as The Dark World of Dreams contained a chapter dedicated to Merratet's dream hunters.

## Metsuke

The metsuke were the secret police of the shogun and a part of his goverment. Their job was primarily to spy on the daimyo and report any insubordination among them. A member of the metsuke would report directly to the roju, the Council of Elders.

There were usually sixteen metsuke at any given time, though the number could vary. The secret police was an honored office, respected by Wa's citizens, like all other offices in the shogun's government. The shogun himself approved anyone serving in the metsuke.

## Mertwig's Maze

Mertwig's Maze is a board game set in the Forgotten Realms setting. Play occurs in the Kingdom of Mertwig, a location invented for the game, in north Faerûn's Vast region.

Gameplay
Players venture into the wilderness, either randomly, towards one of the seven minor boards, or as directed by an event card, in search of the necessary items. Combat with monsters ensues from encounter cards drawn during these explorations. Players also have the option to attack one another in hopes of acquiring royal items from their adversaries. If a character is killed in combat, the player begins the next turn with a new character. Once a player has collected two royal items, they can proceed to the royal palace maze to confront the Mystic Musk-Ox. The player who defeats this final adversary wins the game.

Contents
 1 large paper game map
 8 smaller paper game maps
 190 event cards
 100 diecut, full-color counters
 1 cardstock storage folder
 1 rules booklet

History
Mertwig's Maze was designed by Tom Wham, with editing by Steve Winter, cover art and interior illustrations by Wham, gameboard art by David C. Sutherland III, and graphic coordination by Stephanie Tabat. It was published by TSR, Inc. in 1988, though was originally fully-developed and play-tested by Wham for another game company, Gary Gygax's start-up New Infinities Productions. New Infinities folded before the game could be released and Wham eventually reached out to TSR through friends in the company, managing to sell Jim Ward on it under the caveat that it be somehow tied to Dungeons & Dragons, leading to Mertwig's rework as a Forgotten Realms product. Prior to his contract with New Infinities Productions, Tom Wham had designed an early iteration of the game while he was still an employee at TSR in 1983,Neither the Polyhedron article nor Tom Wham's website state exactly the year of Mertwig's conception, though the latter mentions that it “...lived in a brown cardboard box for about 5 years before getting published”. From this the year 1983 can be extrapolated. under the working title The Dungeon.

Later in 1991, Tom Wham wrote an article titled "More To The Maze" for issue 57 of the Polyhedron Newszine. In this article he provided errata for Mertwig's Maze, a whole new encounter titled "The Tallest Tree In the Forest", and detailed the development history of the board game.

The "Tallest Tree In The Forest" expansion was later uploaded online by Tom Wham to his personal website, alongside a sketch drawn by someone from TSR atop an excerpt of an official map of the Realms to illustrate for him where exactly the Kingdom of Mertwig was located in the Vast.Based on the art style of the map excerpt that was drawn upon and the use of "Ravensgate" instead of "Ravens Bluff", it would appear that the excerpt is of Dennis Kauth's poster map of the Realms from the Forgotten Realms Campaign Set boxed set.

## Merchant's Run

Merchant's Run, sometimes called Merchants Run or the Merchant's Run, was a route connecting the realms of Damara and Impiltur in the Cold Lands. It was both the name of the road along this route as well as the name of the narrow gap between the Dunwood and the Earthspur Mountains through which the road ran.

Geography
Merchant's Run ran southeast from the Damaran city of Trailsend, the capital city of the Barony of Polten, to Ilmwatch in Impiltur on the coast of the Easting Reach. It thus connected the main road in Damara, the King's Road, with the main road in Impiltur, the Herald's Road. Within Impiltur, the stretch of Merchant's Run from the River Lench to Ilmwatch was sometimes considered to be part of the Herald's Road.

The western terminus of the Long Road intersected Merchant's Run in Damara just north of the gap between the Earthspurs and the Dunwood.

Defenses
Within Impiltur's borders, the road was well-patrolled by soldiers from Ilmwatch and had fortified settlements at regular intervals at which travelers could spend the night in well-managed inns.

History
Between the founding of Damara in the late 11 century DR until the mid–14 century DR, the road was a key trade route for merchants bringing bloodstone from the north to sell to lands further south. Long caravans of merchants loaded with gems enjoyed the protection of Impilturan soldiers as they traveled the route before turning south along the Herald's Road to traverse Traders Bay to arrive at ports like Sarshel. The road was the slower but safer, more reliable, and more legitimate route from Damara to Impiltur, the other being to travel down the Great Imphras River.

However, this traffic disappeared with the rise of Zhengyi the Witch-King in the, and his ensuing decade-long war and occupation of Damara. Even after the Witch-King was defeated in the Bloodstone Wars of the, trade was initially slow to resume. It was once again a major trade route by the 1370s DR.

After the water level of the Inner Sea dropped following the events of the Spellplague, the Easting Reach receded southward significantly, and travelers along Merchant's Run were able to proceed passed Ilmwatch across the Easting River all the way to Uthmere, where they could take the Great Road heading further east.

Notable Locations
 Ilmwatch, the southern anchor of the route responsible for patrolling the Impilturan stretch of the road.
 Cairnpur, an overnight stop in Impiltur.
 Maracrath, an overnight stop in Impiltur.
 Guidodale, an overnight stop in Impiltur.
 Mulltown, an overnight stop in Impiltur.
 Lenchford, the crossing point of the River Lench, the border between Impiltur and Damara.
 The Long Road fork, where the road met with the main northern route through Narfell.
 Trailsend, the northern anchor of the route in Damara.

## Mental barrier

Mental barrier was a psionic defense mode and devotion of the telepathy discipline that allowed a manifester to protect their mind with a wall of thought. In the late 14 century DR, this power was reclassified as being part of the clairsentience discipline.

Effects
Mental barriers protected against a psionic blast, but could be penetrated with a psychic crush.

Display
When this power was used it manifested an auditory display.

Users
This power was knowable by psionicists, psychic warriors, wilders, and lurks.

Mental barrier was innately exhibited as a power by amethyst dragons, brain coral, braxat, dhours, hollyphants, hook spiders, ikiryo, observers, the small percentage of psionic quaggoths, ruby dragons, shedu, and topaz dragons.

In psionics terms, it could be known by couatl, duergar, ki-rin,The creatures filed under "in psionics terms" are those who were first stated out with magical powers and rules, which in some cases were described as 'psionics' in fluff text, and then later got stated or revised with actual psionics powers and rules once The Complete Psionics Handbook came out. githyanki and githzerai, and mind flayers, as well as by astral devas, planetars, and solars among aasimon; by amnizu, cornugons, gelugons, and pit fiends among baatezu; by balors, babau, glabrezu, mariliths, nalfeshnee, and yochlol among tanar'ri; and only by arcanaloths among yugoloths.

The duergar goddess Deep Duerra was sometimes known to manifest her influence as a crown-like nimbus of silver light around a creature's head. For the next ten minutes, the target's reservoir of psionic power would quadruple and they would have some kind of psionic defense surrounding them. Mental barrier was among the many defense modes this could be.

Known/Notable Users
 Vhondryl, a psionicist in Skullport and head of the Hall of Mental Splendor

Mental barrier was a commonly exhibited power among the avatars of deities, though a select few stood out for having a far more limited selection of psionic defense modes. The deities who counted mental barrier among their three psionic defenses were as follows: Gruumsh, the Furies, Osiris, Prometheus, Ratri, Skerrit, and Yama.These deities are listed in the cited source as being a Class V psionics user. On page 6 of Deities & Demigods and its repackaged printing Legends & Lore, Class V users are listed as having the Psionic Attack/Defense modes "A,B,C,E/F,G,H". According to the 1st edition Players Handbook, page 110, the defense letters correlate to mind blank, thought shield, and mental barrier respectively.

## Mesku Peak

Mesku Peak was a mountain in the Wu Pi Te Shao Mountains of southern Tabot.

History
In, seven lamas of the Zotung Keep discovered a man frozen in ice on Mesku Peak. They recovered the man and defrosted him, but he melted away with the ice and in his place they found a male leopard cub. They raised the leopard and it grew as big as a horse. Finally, one day it even talked, and asked to be allowed to go free. Surprised, the lamas opened the gates to the leopard, which returned to Mesku Peak. Afterward, the new abbot of the new Zotung Gompa called on the leopard for help when making decisions, and since then, it was said to visit many lamas in dreams to dispense advice on the proper way to achieve enlightenment. The whole affair was declared a miracle, the first of the young nation of Tabot.

A hot spring, claimed to have magical powers, was discovered in the mountains northeast of Mesku Peak in .

## Mei Lung

The Dragon Lord Mei Lung Chen-Shan-Tien-Kung-Te, or simply Mei Lung, was a great lung dragon of the Celestial Empire and the Grand Historian of the Celestial Bureaucracy. He was the creator of the Herald of Mei Lung, a magical tome that told the whole history of the world—right up to the present moment and even into the future.

Description
By the 14 century DR, he had lived over two millennia making him old for a dragon.

Personality
He liked to listen to others and loved history and knowledge, but he valued his privacy intensely.

Abilities
He had some powers over time, which enabled him to see into people's pasts.

Lair
Mei Lung dwelled within the Impossible Palace of the Silver Domes somewhere in the empire of Shou Lung. He relocated it regularly, often to the bed of a deep river or to some other hidden and hard-to-reach place to avoid unwanted visitors, and occasionally to areas facing a grave threat, at which times it was most obvious and accessible. Mei Lung found much of what was written about the Impossible Palace in the Shichi Hsi, the Histories, to be false and fantastical.

Activities
As Grand Historian, he was tasked with keeping a record of past, present, and future events in order that the Celestial Emperor could know all that had transpired and be able to best judge the deeds of humankind. In this capacity, he studied all the peoples and places of Kara-Tur over the course of their history, and journeyed widely to consult with countless other "Chroniclers of Events" to add to his records, as well as the great sages of the Path of Enlightenment.

He was known to write books on history for mortal readers too. The Great Library of Perpustakaan included his account of the creation of the Shao Mountains by the Nine Immortals and the Celestial Emperor, the story of which had been passed down from father to son since Chih Shih himself.

But he could only observe; Mei Lung was forbidden from having direct involvement in the affairs of mortals. Nevertheless, he was permitted to provide information, and even magic and weapons, to those whose need was great and the empire was at risk, if they came to him.

However, Mei Lung did not appreciate trespassers within his palace or those who disturbed his privacy without good reason—in fact, he tended to eat them. Most adventuring companies who tried met this fate. Only those heroes who still dared during a crisis might be met by more than his fangs. To those, Mei Lung gave tests, from games of riddles to battles with dangerous monsters, and sometimes simple conversation on current events or classic works of art and literature, depending on his whims and the situation. Only when he had the measure of a visitor's true nature did he decide whether to aid them, or devour them.

Mei Lung also maintained some position as an ambassador of Shou Lung, which conveniently let him travel around the empire and beyond. He presumably did so in disguise, as dragons of Heaven were rather recognizable.

Relationships

Mei Lung was the seventh son of Men Lung Chen-Shan-Tien-Kung-Te, who was the elder of his clan, and the great-great-grandson of Chih Shih Chen-Shan-Tien-Kung-Te, one of the Nine Immortals and the god of history.

He consulted and befriended a number of sages and scholars, including the poet-sage Hung Te Ping, whom he had maintained a long friendship with, and Elminster of Faerûn, also a great friend of his. He was a firm friend and quite a fan of Tan Hikao Nong the Balladeer, with whom he shared a passion for history and lore.

History
Mei Lung was appointed by the Celestial Emperor himself as the Grand Historian, the 223 since the creation of the world.While Book of Artifacts says Mei Lung was given this role "at the birth of the world", Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms makes it clear Mei Lung is only the 223 Grand Historian, if over 2000 years old. The Book of Artifacts story may represent mortal belief or be mistaken, or else the first Grand Historian had the same name. Ronin Challenge makes it clear Chih Shih was the one at the world's creation. With the end of history itself at stake if he failed, Mei Lung created a back-up to preserve his chronicle—the Herald of Mei Lung.

Moreover, should something terrible happen to him or his household that even he did not foresee, Mei Lung placed the Herald in the custody of the lamas and monks of Ko'Chung Gompa in Tabot some time between  and  when the monasteries were established there. Thereafter, however, Mei Lung had little contact with Tabot and lost track of the place.

The hero Wo Mai once contacted the Tsui Tong to learn the current location of the Impossible Palace, in order to recover the Silver Flower Sword of Tan Ming. Evidently the dragon was caught on a good day, for Mei Lung only chatted with Wo Mai, who survived and later became emperor.

For a time, he posed as an advisor and observer to Ambassador Hing Ti Chin of Shou Lung, with whom he visited the rival empire T'u Lung. There, Mei Lung first met Tan Hikao Nong the Balladeer in Shang Stzi, and then again in the Imperial City, when Nong visited him in his apartments.While it may be reasonable at first glance to assume that the aforementioned Imperial City is Wai, especially given the chapter's focus on T'u Lung, the epithet "Imperial City" is used in all other instances to refer to Kuo Te' Lung. Given that Tan Nong and Mei Lung were both known to travel between Shou Lung and T'u Lung, it is thus more likely that they met in Kuo Te' Lung. But Mei Lung had been following Nong's career much longer, having seen him perform in Karatin and at Sung Tsi's wedding. They became fast friends and later spent a week in Wafang together.

Around 1350 DR, Mei Lung found the young siblings Meo Cha and Meo Li washed up on a beach at Yu Tai Shuni in Shou Lung, having survived the tsunami that destroyed the invasion fleet of the king of Silla. He allowed them to live in his house, where he gladly heard tales of their homeland, Koryo, before they returned there.

Mei Lung came to desire to rediscover Tabot and looked for a chance to return. He got it within a year. Around, Tabot negotiated peace with Shou Lung and invited a delegation to the ceremony at the Temple of Heaven's Heart on Mount E'kwong. As there ceremony was within his jurisdiction, Mei Lung elected to attend in person (though, as a dragon, it seems he was in disguise) with attendants and escorts. He watched the procession of pilgrims for two days, noting people of all ages, warriors, monks, priests, and servants, before the monk Pang introduced himself on the grounds that he looked "important enough" and asked to walk with him up the mountain. On the way, Pang told him much about Tabot, though Mei Lung found him biased and his knowledge lacking, owing to his youth.

Elminster asked Mei Lung for an account of the lands of Kara-Tur, with all its peoples, places, and histories, to be read by another, addressed only as "you" and the Celestial Emperor approved that it be written. Thus, in 2607, Mei Lung wrote up this information, including his own knowledge of Shou Lung, his correspondence with Tan Hikao Nong the Balladeer for T'u Lung, his meeting with Pang and his account of Tabot, and the stories of Koryo from Meo Cha and Meo Li.

Appendix
Background
Mei Lung is the primary narrator of Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms, with the Shou Lung chapter presented from his point of view and three more chapters—T'u Lung, Tabot, and Koryo—introduced by him but narrated by others. However, not all chapters follow this convention: one has a narrator speaking directly to Elminster, others do not make it clear who the narrator is speaking to, and some lack a narrator altogether. This article includes only those chapters with narrators confirmed to be speaking to Mei Lung.

Notes

Appearances
 Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms

## Meld of Lolth

Meld of Lolth was a spell used by priestesses of Lolth.

Effects
When cast, this spell allowed the caster to meld with the mind of another being. This meld gave the caster the ability to not only see through the targeted creature's eyes but also read their thoughts and could even communicate telepathically. The spell was not affected by distance, however the connection was broken if the targeted creature was transported to another plane.

The spell also gave the caster the ability to dominate the targeted creature. The caster was also capable of casting a new spell through the enspelled individual. While the spell was in effect, the caster could move and talk normally and cast additional spells. If the targeted creature was killed, the caster risked suffering from system shock, which could kill them instantly.

The spell was often used by the priestesses of Lolth to control powerful and strong drow males before battle.

Components
This spell required verbal and somatic components.

Appendix
References

## Memory read

Memory read was a common divine spell that allowed the cleric to look into a creature's mind.

Effects
The spell's target was a single creature, and it could be cast from a distance of . When cast successfully, memory read let the caster and the target experience a specific memory with the same clarity and intensity as it was preserved in the target's mind. It took the cleric time equal to one-fifteenth of the actual event's length to read the memory. The spell worked well on sleeping or unconscious individuals, as well as those under the influence of hold or paralyzation magic.

This spell could only affect creatures with intelligence greater than that of a beast, and the cast had to remain in close range or the duration of the reading. Even the sleeping target had a chance of resisting the memory read spell. The target could attempt to hide a memory or certain aspects from the cleric. The spell became more changing the older the target memory was.

Memory read took a toll on the caster. Mental strain exerted during spellcasting affected the priest's constitution, and the caster was unable to re-cast the spell instill they received full eight hours of sleep or reverie, restoring their health.

This spell was among those that could be cast by priests while underwater or by aquatic spellcasters.

Components
The spell required verbal, somatic, and material components components. The material component was a small piece of linen cloth with thread-of-gold strewn in. The spell consumed the material component once cast.

Notable Users
Organizations
Mazewalkers, the specialty clerics of Chultan deity Ubtao, were granted the ability to memory read or use the spell of addition once per day.
Tyr's holy justices were granted the ability to memory read after reaching a certain rank.

Individuals
Amaunator's avatar had the ability to look into minds of criminals, in a way that closely resembled the memory read spell.
Denton Crimsguard, a vocal priest of Tyr from Alaghôn circa 1370 DR.

Other
Flame of the Spirit, a holy artifact of the Tymoran faith. Occasionally, the goddess granted individuals who held the item access to spells, including memory read.

Detect thoughts

Circa the, the Merchants Guild was locked in a game of politics with the Lord of Arrabar Eles Wianar. The guild was fiercely fighting to retain its independence by electing their own successor to the title of the Guildmaster of Merchants, while Lord Wianar sought to install his son, claiming additional ruling power in the city.

## Memphiria

Memphiria was the divine domain of the Pharaonic and Mulhorandi deity of death, Osiris.

Cosmology
In the Great Wheel cosmology, Memphiria was one of the three triangular divine realms that made up Heliopolis. Under the World Tree cosmology, it was likewise part of Heliopolis, which in that cosmology was a neutral plane that was shared by all members of the Mulhorandi pantheon.
Planar Traits
In the Great Wheel cosmology, undead were deprived of their special innate abilities while in Memphiria. For example, the mummies of this realm did not produce mummy rot.

Geography
In both cosmologies Memphiria was a desert realm, though in the Great Wheel it had a cool and chilly temperature to it. It also included both a surface area and an underground area, the latter reffered to as the "Underworld".

Notable Locations
 Memphiria: A residential city built of white marble, which was rather lacking in terms of goods and services. In the World Tree cosmology, it was said to be a grand necropolis surrounded by rows of mausoleums.

Inhabitants
In the Great Wheel cosmology, this divine realm was also primarily inhabited by petitioners. They moved slowly about their business and weren't as lively as the petitioners elsewhere in Heliopolis, but were still largely content with their lot in life. Underground, the spirits of kings who ruled wisely in life ruled over the spirits that had served them in life.

Beyond petitioners, his realm in the Great Wheel was inhabited by mummies (though they were not evil) and a variety of other undead creatures that were not of evil alignment. The mummies of this realm would rise up to seek vengeance upon those who despoiled their graves, while other undead came to the realm in order to complete some form of quest or out of a desire to reclaim some semblance of a normal, mortal life. The petitioners of this realm treated the undead no different from the living, through they would fearlessly attack any undead that was evil.

In the World Tree cosmology, his realm was said to be inhabited solely by petitioners of Mulhorandi origin, though these petitioners were all non-evil corporeal forms of undead.
Notable Inhabitants
 Khallis Mhektis: The fair, wise, and impartial half-elf proxy of Osiris who ruled over the city of Memphiria. They had a sorrowful look to them and their only motivation was to serve Osiris well.

## Metallic peacekeeper

Metallic Peacekeepers were the humanoid variety of metallic sentinels, constructs created by metallic dragons to watch over and keep safe weaker beings that the benevolent dragons cared for.

Description
Like all sentinels, peacekeepers were created from fine, filigreed metal, of the variety associated with their master. Similarly, they were powered by the type of energy associated with their creator's breath weapon.

Abilities
These beings possessed an innate, telepathic bond with their draconic creators. They could communicate in the common and draconic languages. This bond could be replicated with another being with the use of a mimic battery.

Combat
Metallic peacekeepers made simple attacks with their fists, but preferred an alternate method to subdue aggrieved foes. They could release a soothing mist in an area with a radius of  centered upon themself.

Their physical forms could not be harmed by fire or altered in any way, even by magical means.

Like other constructs they were immune to charm spells and effects, did not succumb to exhaustion, and could not be poisoned or frightened.

History
The Abeiran dragonborn artificers of the Inquest maintained a collection of ten metallic peacekeepers within the Supreme Forge in the city of Xorvintroth.

The spell could only affect a willing creature. The creature became incapable of recalling a specific message imparted unto it until conditions selected by the caster were met. Per some sources, however, the spell might not have been completely unimpeachable, and mages whose power overwhelmed that of the original caster may have been able to devise methods of cracking this spell.

## Mermaid's breath

Mermaid's breath was an alchemical draught used to revive the drowned.

When applied into the mouth of a recently drowned person (no later than four minutes), it gave them a chance at reviving.

History
The synthesis of this concoction was extremely delicate, requiring both chemical and herbal knowledge in order to brew correctly. Hedge wizards often traded in it, stocking up if they were near bodies of water. It sold for about 1000 gold pieces.

## Messenger of Sseth

The Messenger of Sseth was a powerful enchanted Ssethssar longbow.

Description
The Messenger of Sseth was visually distinct. The bow's grip was banded with red and black – colors of the yuan-ti archer Apali's pious priest family. The bow's ends were carved to depict snake heads, symbolizing the yuan-ti deity Sseth.

Powers
The longbow was a powerful +2 enchanted weapon that allowed the wielder to launch three arrows per six seconds.

History
This bow was crafted thousands of years before the, by a warm-blood hater named Apali. The yuan-ti was a powerful and long-living archer who slew countless men, women, and children with the Messenger of Sseth over the course of several centuries he lived through. Apali was killed in Chessenta by a cleric of Assuran in revenge for the murder of the cleric's great-grandparent in Chult a century earlier.

The bow changed hands until it reached the Dragon's Eye on the Spine of the World. In 1281 DR, the Messenger of Sseth was wielded by a yuan-ti High Archer in service of a marilith Yxunomei.

## Merchant elemental

Merchant elementals, also known by the name of their creator, Leomund's merchant elementals, were a special variety of elemental creatures that could perform functions of a shopkeep.

Description
Merchant elementals were strange beings that bore an uncanny resemblance to the individual who summoned or created them. They looked like animated and intelligent people made of stone. The elemental was placed on a mage's circle and emitted slight steam.

An alternative to Leomund's merchant elemental was Aphelda's vendor golem. However, the golem was an utter mercantile pushover.

Behavior
These creatures were stoic, quiet, and behaved like competent store owners, performing all tasks a real shopkeep did on a daily basis. They could purchase, sell, and haggle over the store's stock. Skilled wizard store keeps could be sure that Leomund's merchant elemental left in charge of their stores did an adequate job. Those who witnessed Leomund's merchant elementals claimed that the creatures had understanding and empathetic facial expressions and had a keen business sense.

Ecology
Merchant elementals were not part of the natural way of things. They did not need to nourish themselves nor needed sleep.

History
Leomund's merchant elementals were extremely rare, and it was unclear if they were summoned creatures or crafted and animated. Those who knew this secret did not feel the need to divulge that information. In the, one such creature served elven shop owner and wizard Sand of Neverwinter. The elf entrusted his merchant elemental to run the shop while Sand joined Kalach-Cha during the Shadow War.

## Memory wrack

Memory wrack was a wicked divine spell from the thought sphere that affected a target's ability to retain information.

Effects
This spell temporarily disconnected the target from their short term and long term memory, preventing them from remembering any information for its duration. The spell did not affect memories that occurred before the spell was cast, but any events occurring after the casting were forgotten by the target within seconds.

Memory wrack also prevented the target from formulating any complex plans, limiting them to simple solitary actions. However, any break in the target's concentration would cause them to forget what they were doing or attempting to do. Once the spell expired, the target had no recollection of anything that transpired while the spell was active.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component a ruby worth at least 200 gp to cast. The ruby was crushed during the casting.

History
Sensates, who were specialty priests devoted to Sharess, used the memory wrack spell.

Appendix
References

## Metastakixx

Metastakixx was a strange creature that was created as a sentient guardian untold millennia before the 14 century DR by a forgotten deity.

Description

Metastakixx was a mysterious and alien creature wrapped in a humanoid and somewhat feminine winged form. Their skin was a chrome-like mirror, and their body was shrouded in glowing light, worn like clothes. Metastakixx stood in the middle of the temple, unmoving like a metallic statue with intelligence sparking from behind their cloudy eyes.

Those who saw Metastakixx struggled with keeping their eyes on the creature, finding the winged form hard to comprehend, as if subconsciously making a discussion not to risk looking at their reflective shape for too long. Observers of the strange statuesque being felt an uncontrollable, overwhelming sorrow as if their hearts wept. When they spoke, they radiated waves of sadness and melancholy.

Personality

Metastakixx's mind was affected by their unknowable age and seeming constant pain. They could not speak easily as each word was a tremendous raspy-sounding effort. When Metastakixx had enough strength to try and remember anything, it was clear that everything in the creature's memory was gone, if it ever was there. Metastakixx did show signs of sentience and desire to know that or who they were and hoped to one day find out. The creature did not know of the "world." They have never seen what lies outside of their prison.

Possessions
The temple where Metastakixx stood was hidden in an ancient cave deep in the cliffs of the Crab's Claw Coast. The small domed temple rose from behind an ancient ornamental gate in a flooded cavern on an island. The chamber was filled with shadow-like mists. Unknown to all but the guardian, the mists were Fragments of a Corrupt God, a form of primordial evil suspended in a temporary form to be sealed away and rendered harmless. The shadows were kept in a harmless state by strange floating globes – Mirrored Vaporizers that projected rays of red light up towards the temple's dome.

The hidden temple held several artifacts that held echoes of the lost gods. The Last Remaining Pillar was a column that radiated old memories. When touched, bygone echoes of indeterminate gender sounded directionless in one's mind. The voice whispered, "Sssshhhhhh, we are sleeeeeeping...", then another voice joined in, joined by another, until it was a mental roar "Don't  lissssten, free usss, desssstroy them!"

Strange Sea Creature Statues decorated the temple. The creatures had some resemblance to dolphins but were somehow different, making it apparent that the depicted creatures were likely long lost to the world. Like other things in the temple, the "dolphins" projected a mental message "Whisperers in the darkness. Ancient evil. Hideous hissing. Violence unchained. Peaceful shores under a silver sky. So it shall be, so has it has ever been."

A strange ancient artifact, the Dreamstone, that dated back to the goddess Mystryl, could be found on the temple's floor, possibly a remnant of some unfortunate soul trapped in the temple.

Lastly, the creature's shimmering glowing clothes were an enchanted cloak - the unique Cloak of Mists.

Relationships
Metastakixx remembered almost nothing from their past apart from the name of a single being destined to release them from the unending sentry duty. The mirror figure did not remember how they knew of that person, nor did they ever wonder how they knew that name. Without Metastakixx and the destined individual knowing it, in some strange way, they've always loved each other, long before one was born and long after the other lost all of their memory.

History

Everything about Metastakixx was a mystery. They knew that they were not born nor trapped in the lost chamber. Instead, Metastakixx was created inside the prison and tasked with the sentry duty over Fragments of a Corrupt God. It was unknown if Metastakixx was a woman, despite their feminine shape, and it was unknown if they were created to be sentient or if the sentience was developed over the course of countless centuries Metastakixx stood guard. "Forever" by their own accord.

Metastakixx waited for their creators to return, but they never did. The winged being stood guard, unmoving as centuries passed. Memories of who and what the masters were became clouded and then forgotten, just like the world around the temple forgot the creators. Memories disappeared one after the other until nothing remained in Metastakixx's mind but the sentry duty and the name of one individual not yet born.

Occasionally, moments of lucidity sparked in Metastakixx's mind, and they remembered ancient evil–shadows that came to swallow everything. Metastakixx's maker was in danger, and the sentry sealed the shadows, these  Fragments of a Corrupt God. The mirror being was tasked with keeping the shadows sealed in the hidden prison until the makes returned. They never did.

In the late 14 century DR, an ancient key that unlocked the gates to Metastakixx's lost temple could be found in a pile of junk in Torleth's Treasures curio store in the hamlet of Gillian's Hill.

Those who entered Metastakixx's chamber became trapped by the creature. The temple became sealed off until the mirrored being was released from their infinite task or the trapped Fragments of a Corrupt God were secured from being unleashed on the world. Metastakixx waited for the individual whose name was etched in their mind, the one who could defeat the shadows and help Metastakixx fulfill their purpose.

Unbeknown to Metastakixx, keeping the shadows imprisoned was the only thing, the only purpose, that sustained the winged guardian's life. If released, Metastakixx died, possibly due to expending all of their strength over the millennia or because the magical prison and Metastakixx were one and the same. The individual whose name Metastakixx remembered was destined to release them, leaving the savior swallowed by sadness and grief, crying over Metastakixx's lifeless body.

## Mend-nets

Mend-nets was a warrior of the Ice Hunters tribe that made their home on Oyaviggaton during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
He was a rather untrusting individual that held a distinct distaste for the monstrous creatures that laired within his tribe's icy island-home. He detested the kobolds that laired within the isle's cave network, loathed its community of revolting ice toads, and became completely frightened by its ravenous ice troll denizens.

History
Sometime during or before the 1480s DR, Mend-nets became infected with some sort of disease. He was segregated from the rest of his tribes in the monster-filled caves that wound throughout Oyaviggaton, to both keep him warm and help protect his fellow Ice Hunters.

## Meltharond

Meltharond was a once-powerful wizard-ruler  and former consort and companion of the white dragon Arveiaturace.

Relationships
At some point in their relationship, Meltharond was considered to be Arveiaturace's master. The white dragon served as his aerial steed, and he instructed her in the arcane arts. They became close friends, lovers, and in fact, Meltharond was the only being that Arveiaturace ever truly confided to.

History
Meltharond was once the ruler of Ice Peak, a frigid island south of the Sea of Moving Ice.

He died at some point during or before the, but his body stayed with Arveiaturace for over a century afterwards. The white dragon refused to accept Meltharond's death, and his frigid remains were bound to his saddle strapped on her back.

## Merfolk language

The Merfolk language or Merman language was the language spoken by merfolk.

Speakers
Beyond merfolks themselves, Merfolk was a common secondary language among sea elves and asrai, water sprites of the Upper Planes.

Some of the individual specialty priests of aquatic deities were known to learn various other aquatic languages, such as Merfolk. These included the sea druids of Deep Sashelas, the waveservants of Umberlee, and the waterwalkers of Istishia.

Derivative Languages
Serusan, a pidgin language used by the various aquatic races of Serôs, featured elements of the merfolk language.

Magic
The spell aura of the sea would automatically translate any spoken Merfolk into Common for those also standing within the aura.

Merfolk Dictionary
A
Amtola
 "traveler".
H
Heumixumlis
The merfolk term for anguiliians. Translated literally, it meant "eel man from the deep".
S
Serôs
 The undersea realms of the Inner Sea. Translates literally as "the embracing life".

Notable Speakers
 Ashakar Piscelen, a triton wizard in Ravens bluff, was capable of speaking this language.
 Clark Pebble, a half-elf fighter and former sea captain, was capable of speaking this language.
 Kladdin Frivil, a human swashbuckler and wizard in Ravens bluff, was capable of speaking this language.

Those standing within the aura of the aura of the sea spell would hear merfolk language translated into Common.

## Merchants' Guild (Daggerford)

The Merchants' Guild was the guildhall for the various merchants and businesspeople in the city of Daggerford in the Western Heartlands in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
The guildhall was located where the southern arm of Rogar Alley met the High Road in central Daggerford near the drill field. It was one of the larger buildings found along the High Road.

Description
The first floor of this building was cut out as if it were a basement, while the second floor was used as the primary meeting hall. The guildhall was haphazardly decorated with statues, pottery, and carvings–none of which blended well with any of the other decor.

Activities
Meetings held here between the various merchants of Daggerford with the hopes of gaining a communal influence over the city council was mostly a waste of time given the merchants' inherent competitive nature against each other.

Merchant's glamer was a spell used by clerics of Vergadain.

When cast, this spell altered the appearance of material goods so that they appeared to be of finer quality then they actually were. The illusion was powerful enough that only spells that penetrate illusion spells such as detect illusion or true seeing could detect the alterations. The spell lasted a full day or when all the ensorcelled goods were sold. At that point, the true nature of the items was revealed. Clerics of Vergadain rarely, if ever, used this spell when dealing with other dwarves.

## Meldryn Jalensifer

Meldryn Jalensifer was the Magister from  to .

History
Meldryn came upon the idea to slay the previous Magister after looting a stronghold of the Cult of the Dragon northwest of the Vilhon Reach, discovering spells that could create skeletal dragons and zombie dragons. He lured Constulgrael to the Howling Caverns, where he had laid down zombie and skeleton dragons, and then struck the Magister with spells and summoned monsters when he appeared. Eventually, Meldryn slew the dragon.

Once he had the office of the Magister, Meldryn created a number of constructs to speak for him, dedicating all of his time to hiding and misdirecting mages who sought to challenge him. Even though Mystra pointedly gave him the scepter of Mystra in  with orders to give it to a minor mage, he performed that action and immediately returned to hiding. Mystra reminded him in dreams and visions that the Magister was supposed to spread magic; in response, he wrote down dozens of copies of Jalensifer's Trident and went on a whirlwind tour to spread them.

He was eventually ambushed in his hidden lair in the Star Mounts by a challenger, but rather than attack, he responded with visible relief, and asked only for a few days in order to explain his situation at leisure instead of running and hiding. Though suspicious, his challenger accepted. A few days later, Sarndaen Dalabar, the challenger, slew the Magister with three meteor swarms in quick succession, just to make sure.

Rumors & Legends
Meldryn's ability to stay undetected was excellent, leading some to suspect a psionic wild talent was involved.

## Memories of Kuldahar

Memories of Kuldahar was an enchanted club fashioned out of a massive branch of the Great Oak of Kuldahar and believed to be a gift from Silvanus, the Oak Father.

Powers
Memories of Kuldahar carried the Great Oak's magical warmth, protecting its wielder from cold. The same nature magics made the club a powerful enchanted +1 weapon.

History
In the, the town of Kuldahar was besieged by forces of the baatezu Belhifet that poured over the Spine of the World, being fueled by the foul magics of Crenshinibon. Under the onslaught of evil, the Great Oak was battered by the fierce ice winds of the Frozenfar, reducing the tree's magical circle of warmth that sheltered Kuldahar. During these storms, several branches were snapped off the Great Oak. After the siege was over, these branches were collected by the town's inhabitants and kept in their homes as a symbol of Kulpahar's perseverance in the face of grave odds. With time, several of the branches found their way to the Ten Towns, and one became a magical club known as Memories of Kuldahar.

At one point before Mirtul of the, Memories of Kuldahar was in stock of the Gallaway Trade Depot and available for purchase from Deirdre Gallaway in Targos.

In the, Memories of Kuldahar ended up in the grabby hands of one of the verbeegs under Slabhead's command, who herded a tribe of snow goblins in the Fallen Crags of Icewind Dale in attempts to stop any Ten-Towners from passing through the area.

## Metwell Greenwood

History
Metwell was born in a humble family of fishers on the Gulf of Lath of Lantan. He was a short man and the weakest of his family. As a child, Metwell spent his time hauling catch, gutting fish, and mopping the boat's deck. By the age of eighteen, he grew disgusted with fish and decided to become a priest of the god of forests Silvanus. It was an act of teenage rebellion that led him to an adventuring career, far, far away from the much-maligned smell and slime of fish.

Sometime in the 14 century DR, Greenwood was among adventurers interested in being sent into a mysterious dungeon by an unnamed sorceress to retrieve the equally mysterious magical orb from its depths.
